### Compact print additions (v1.25.0 + v1.25.1 — main-course extraction)

The compact menu print appends what a main course comes with **on the same line**, extracted from the official description. Owner rulings locked over 2026-09-24/25/26 (digest v1.3 review + preview rounds):

- **One line.** Dish name verbatim, uppercased, always first; additions appended behind it in grey (`.pc-add`, `#555`). Never a second line.
- **Ẃ everywhere (U+1E82, capital w-acute).** The connector word "with" is replaced by `Ẃ` in main-course names **and** as the addition connector. Parts join with `AND`. Chosen to match the all-caps card; lowercase `ẃ` (U+1E83) was rejected.
- **v1.25.1 — no double Ẃ (owner ruling 2026-09-26).** When the dish name itself contains "with" (already rendered `Ẃ`), the addition's connector is **AND** instead of a second `Ẃ`: `BAKED FISH Ẃ HERB SAUCE AND ROASTED POTATOES`, `EGG OMELETTE STUFFED Ẃ MUSHROOMS AND PORK SAUSAGES AND POTATOES`. (Owner picked AND over PLUS, — and comma.)
- **v1.25.2 — no AND pileup (owner ruling 2026-09-26).** When the name contains **"and"** (with or without a "with"), the connector is `Ẃ`: `BRAISED CHICKEN Ẃ BABY ABALONE AND MOREL MUSHROOM Ẃ STEAMED RICE`. Full rule: **connector = AND only when the name has "with" and no "and"; otherwise Ẃ** (`/\bwith\b/i && !/\band\b/i` on the raw name). Corpus: 68 mains have both words (16 composed lines flipped), 302 with-only (190 keep AND), 73 and-only + 696 neither (already Ẃ).
- **Foreign-name protein reveals** render as `— PROTEIN` (PMOD-stripped bare noun) directly after the name, before the Ẃ flow: `SUKIYAKI — BEEF Ẃ STEAMED RICE`. G-class reveals are **KEPT** (English protein hint); sauce-level traces are **IGNORED** (never counted, never displayed).
- **Mains only** (`printIsMainCourse` = `/main|entr[eé]e|entree/i` on the course label). Other courses, drinks, delectables and light bites are untouched. No match → no addition (dish prints exactly as before, except the name's WITH→Ẃ which applies to all mains).
- Protein words invert (`.pc-hl`) even inside additions; the canvas export mirrors this via `compactItemTokens` (`add`-flagged tokens draw `#555`).

**Code:** `compactExtractAddition` / `compactComposeAddition` + `EX_*` vocabularies in `index.html` (just above `printCompactItems`). Extraction is two axes over the description: **starch** (`EX_STARCH_ALT` + `EX_STARCH_MOD` modifiers + pudding/cake/hash/crust/bites suffixes; winner = last candidate with a connector (`EX_CONN`) within 45 chars before it — skipping one whose last word repeats an earlier candidate's (Yaki Onigiri's `rice` vs `Grilled rice`), else the first candidate; a starch word in the NAME suppresses the starch) and **protein** (`EX_PALT` + `EX_PMOD`; phrase allows one adjacent protein token so "pork sausages"/"cod roes" hold together; saucey compounds (`EX_SAUCEY` — chicken broth etc.) masked first; a protein preceded by "the" is the dish itself, not an ingredient; exact-case dedupe; connector within 45 chars before the hit = **hidden** (full phrase in the Ẃ/AND flow, before the starch), else **primary** (bare noun after the —); a protein word in the NAME suppresses the meat axis). Composition order: `— PRIMARY1 AND PRIMARY2`, then `Ẃ HIDDEN1 AND HIDDEN2 AND STARCH` — except the flow connector follows the v1.25.1/v1.25.2 rules above (`AND` only when the raw name has "with" and no "and"). Display dedupes by core protein word (`prawns`≡`prawn`; first, richest phrase wins — `Bak Chor Mee` reads `Ẃ MINCED PORK`, not `Ẃ MINCED PORK AND PORK`).

**Contract:** `tests/menu-extraction.fixture.json` freezes all **1,139 unique (name, description) pairs** from the official main-course corpus (`_inbox/menu/sq_main_courses.json`) with expected starch winner, meat list (phrase + style) and composed line — validated pair-by-pair against the owner-reviewed digest v1.3 (`_inbox/menu/extraction-digest.md`, untracked; regenerate, don't commit). `tests/menu-extraction.test.js` sweeps the fixture, pins the owner-approved signature lines (tiger prawns / sweet & sour fish / omelette / sukiyaki / braised chicken with+and), the WITH→Ẃ name rule, the connector rules, mains-only scoping, canvas tokens and both version stamps. **A fixture mismatch means extraction drifted** — regenerating it is a deliberate act that ships in the same commit with a reason. (Regenerated for v1.25.1: 202 composed lines changed for the AND rule; for v1.25.2: 16 more flipped back to Ẃ for and-names; starch/meat axes untouched both times.)

**Known cosmetic deviations from the digest's section labels (rendering identical, winners + meats 100 %):** 2 A/B label diffs (Chicken Mayo Sandwich, Spiced Beef Short Rib — both would need "on" as a connector, which Wagyu Beef Burger contradicts) and 6 C→D diffs (name-starch words outside the frozen ALT list, none with description candidates). **Owner display rulings (v1.28.3, applied):** list joins read as a comma list WITHOUT a serial comma — `KALGUKSU — ANCHOVY Ẃ CLAMS, PRAWNS, SCALLOP AND WHEAT NOODLES` (two items stay `A AND B`); ONE dedupe across both groups keyed on the phrase's first protein word, the LONGEST wording surviving in the FIRST mention's slot — `WANTON MEE Ẃ PORK CHAR SIEW`, `KHAO THOM KAI — CHICKEN MEAT Ẃ FRAGRANT JASMINE RICE`; multi-starch stays one-starch-last-connector-hit (ruled keep — flipping to first-hit changes 42 of 109 lines and cuts both ways: rice beats the japchae sweet potato on Dak Ggochi Gui, but the thalis' named Aloo components would wrongly win over rice). The unified key also un-swallows wrongly-collapsed phrases (`SHRIMP` beside `PORK SHRIMPS`, `DELICATE CHICKEN` over plain `CHICKEN`) and promotes the real `FRESH PRAWNS` over Hokkien Mee's broth prawn; the one information fold is the donburi's `SALMON ROE` into `SALMON` (same core, flagged to the owner). 43 composed lines changed; the fixture was regenerated in the same commit (starch/meat axes untouched). Ẃ sits in Plus Jakarta Sans's latin-ext subset (Google's latin-ext covers U+1E00–U+1E9F), so it renders in the print font; if a future font swap drops it, the per-glyph system fallback is near-identical at 6 px.

### Offline support (v1.26.0 — saved menus, status dot, hardened shell)

Owner scenario: look up a flight's menu pre-departure (all cabins), airplane mode, close the app, reopen onboard — the menu must still be there. Shipped 2026-09-26:

- **Saved menus.** `executeFetchCabins` caches the getcabin payload per flight+date (`sqCabinCacheKey` = `SQ<flight>:<date>:CABINS`); per-cabin menu payloads already cached by `loadMenu`/`cacheMenu`/`fetchMenuForPrinter` under `SQ<flight>:<date>:<cabin>`. Offline (`!navigator.onLine`) or fetch-failure → the flight card renders from the saved schedule (`useSaved` param; the catch retries once from the save before erroring — covers captive portals), then the viewer/printer serve the per-cabin caches via the pre-existing stale path. `renderMenu(data, isStale, savedAtTs)` now stamps the badge: "Offline — menu saved 26 Sep, 09:41" (`#menu-offline-badge-txt`). Within the 6 h TTL a cached menu renders with NO badge (it is fresh). Honest refusals: offline + nothing saved → amber notice in the card; un-viewed cabin offline → appAlert; printer offline + nothing → dedicated message (openMenuPrinter catch).
- **Status dot.** The brand dot (`#net-status-dot`) is live: green `animate-pulse` online, steady `bg-amber-500` offline (online/offline events + `updateNetStatus`). Tap → `#net-status-pop` popover (v1.26.1: `glass-sheet` solid surface — chat must not show through — and a 200 ms fade+scale in/out via `opacity-0 scale-95` + `origin-top-left`, with a close-timer guard against reopen races): status line, "Rates last checked …" (`crewAssist.ratesCheckedAt`, stamped by `loadShippedRates` on a successful network fetch), capability list (calculators/earnings/roster import always ✓, saved menus = flights with a CABINS cache, live lookups ✗ offline). Popover closes on outside tap/Escape.
- **Storage hygiene.** `pruneSqMenuCache()` (called from `rememberRecentFlight`) drops every `SQ…:…:…` key not belonging to the 5 recent flights. `requestPersistentStorage()` runs at boot (`navigator.storage.persist()`; Android honours it, iOS home-screen apps are exempt from the 7-day sweep anyway).
- **SW hardening.** `sw.js` v135 precaches the Tailwind CDN script + pdf.js + worker (CDN_ASSETS, individually `cache.add().catch()`-guarded so a CDN hiccup can't break install). Fixes the first-offline-launch gap where the shell served but Tailwind/pdf.js had never runtime-cached.
- **Tests:** `tests/offline-menus.test.js` (29 asserts at v1.26.0; 33 at v1.27.1 after the share-target block left with the feature) — schedule reader, offline card refusal + render, viewer badge, fresh-cache no-badge, printer stale fallback + honest error, prune, dot colours + popover, persist request, stamps. Harness fix that came with it: jsdom's window has no `Response` constructor, so the fetch stub now builds replies with Node's global `Response` (the /api/sq stub replies 500 instead of throwing — no suite asserted on the old rejection text). The backup nudge's manifest/source asserts live here too since v1.27.0; the Android share-target block that briefly joined this suite was **removed in v1.27.1** along with the feature (see Recovery).

**Save dedupe (v1.28.1):** both save paths (single summary `doSave` and the Calculate-all bulk button) go through `persistArchiveSaving(payloads)`. An entry's signature = `monthKey|sectorDate|flightType|stationDisplay` — same signature means the same duty, so a re-attach + re-save (the normal workflow since v1.28.0's roster memory) **replaces** the earlier entry with the new amount/savedAt instead of stacking a duplicate. The single-save ack says "updated" vs "saved"; the bulk ack appends "(N updated)". Turnaround vs layover on the same day are different signatures and stay separate. Reported by the owner after re-attaching Sep–Nov rosters over saved earnings.

**v1.35.0 (owner request, the versioned changelog):** What's New now shows **the delta** — every version newer than the last one the crew saw, nothing older, never a replay. `APP_WHAT_NEW` (flat, current-release-only) is replaced by `APP_CHANGELOG`: newest-first `{ v, items[] }` entries covering **every release since inception** (v1.5.9 → current, ~88 versions, distilled from this file). Mechanics: `wnSeenVersion()` reads `crewAssist.wnSeen` (written the moment the sheet OPENS — closing without the checkbox no longer replays it on every launch, the "past features" report); a missing stamp means a pre-1.35.0 device that has seen everything before this update, so the floor is the release before current (the multi-version delta math works for every FUTURE jump: 1.29.0 → 1.35.1 shows 1.29.1..1.35.1). "Do not show again" now writes `hideWhatsNew = '1'` — suppressed for good, the changelog is the catch-up. The list renders **grouped with version headers** (one `renderChangelogInto` renderer) and **scrolls** (`max-h-[50vh] overflow-y-auto`) when a big jump brings many entries; the title reads `v1.29.1 → v1.35.0` for multi-version jumps. New **Settings → Learn the app → Changelog** opens a bottom-sheet overlay with the whole history, newest first, plus "You are on vX" — extend it by appending one entry at the top of `APP_CHANGELOG` each release (the release checklist's WN step now means: new entry + delta rules hold; the v1.29.1 no-accumulate ruling lives on inside the entry you append). `APP_VERSION` became `var` (test-reachable, like `printState`/`currentMenuSearch`). dev-ui E6 rewritten for the structure (≥60 entries, strictly descending, newest === APP_VERSION, current-release pointers ≤12 words) plus a behaviour block (fresh stamp → one release; 1.29.0 stamp → 17 headers, scrollable; up-to-date → no sheet; Settings overlay reaches v1.5.9). Tests: 11 suites / 1326 asserts / 0 fail; sw v161.
**v1.35.0 hotfix (owner order: NO version bump — still 1.35.0, sw v162):** *What’s New showed once and went away without the crew tapping “Do not show again”.* The v1.35.0 stamp-on-open was wrong — the sheet must RETURN on every launch while a delta is pending, and only the checkbox dismisses it. Opening/closing no longer writes anything; the checkbox writes BOTH `wnSeen` and `hideWhatsNew = APP_VERSION` (per-version hide — a future release shows again, the original pre-1.35.0 semantics — NOT the ‘1’ forever-stamp that shipped an hour earlier). dev-ui 223 pins: open leaves no stamp, close-unticked returns, ticked dismissal keeps it away and stamps seen. Tests: 11 suites / 1332 asserts / 0 fail; sw v162.

**v1.35.0 hotfix 2 (owner order: NO version bump — still 1.35.0, sw v163):** `APP_CHANGELOG` grew to **113 entries covering every release since inception (v1.0.0 → v1.35.0)**. The owner supplied the pre-v1.5.9 history in chat (2026-10-01) — 25 entries, v1.0.0 → v1.5.8, embedded below the v1.5.9 sign-off (see **Pre-history** at the end of the version history) — and every v1.5.9 → current entry was expanded to **multiple pointers, one sentence each** (owner's pointer ruling: one pointer, one sentence; more pointers per version encouraged). The v1.35.0 entry itself keeps four pointers, all ≤12 words. dev-ui: the Settings-changelog reach-back assert now pins **v1.0.0** (was v1.5.9); E6 passes unchanged (113 ≥ 60, strictly descending, newest === APP_VERSION, 1.35.0 pointers ≤12 words); offline-menus + menu-extraction stamp asserts moved to sw v163. Tests: 11 suites / 1355 asserts / 0 fail; sw v163.

**v1.35.0 hotfix 3 (owner spec, no version bump — still 1.35.0, sw v164):** the Settings changelog sheet is **redesigned**. Layout top-to-bottom: header (title, rocket + installed version in gold, circular close) → controls row (**All / New / Fixes / Improvements** filter chips, horizontally scrollable, active chip gold-filled; sort toggle flipping newest ↔ oldest via arrow-down/up-wide-narrow) → a gold-tinted **What’s new for you** card (party-popper badge; “You missed N updates in vX” from the `wnSeen` delta, or “all caught up”; pills count new features vs improvements) → the scrollable grouped list → bottom safe-area padding. **Version headers are sticky** (backdrop blur over the sheet surface) with the version in gold, a pulsing **NEW** badge on unseen versions, **CURRENT** on the running version, and a **relative date** (Today / Yesterday / N days ago / Month Year). **Rows carry category tags** — NEW (sparkles) / FIX (bug) / IMPROVE (wrench) / FUN (smile; the lighthearted menu rulings) — each with a gold bullet dot, a per-category tint, press highlight and hover behind `@media (hover:hover)`. Groups fade/slide in (staggered ≤350ms); a filter with no matches shows a search-x empty state. **Data change:** every pointer is now `{ c, t }` (categorised new/fix/imp/fun) and every version since **v1.5.9** carries `d` — an approximate release date derived from the development log (owner-approved: 88 dated versions, 25 undated pre-history ones). What’s New keeps `renderChangelogInto` (plain lines, reads `.t`); the sheet renders via `renderChangelogSheet` with `var changelogState` (filter/sort, reset on every open; test-reachable). The v1.35.0 entry gained its last pointer: “Changelog sheet redesigned: category filters, sort toggle, and a highlights card.” Tests: dev-ui E6 rewritten for the object shape (categories + date coverage machine-checked) plus a redesign behaviour block (badges, sticky headers, filter, sort, empty state, summary card); 11 suites / 1381 asserts / 0 fail; sw v164.

**v1.35.0 hotfix 4 (owner orders, no version bump — still 1.35.0, sw v165):** (1) *Changelog pointers are non-tappable* — the rows’ press highlight and hover tint are gone (they were never buttons; this supersedes the hotfix-3 spec’s “rows highlight subtly on press” line). (2) *The list sits flush under the What’s-new-for-you card* — the scroll container’s top padding is removed, so the first version header and every sticky header while scrolling sit directly against the card. (3) *The pre-history is dated* — the owner supplied the real inception timeline (first commit 4 Sep 2026: PWA, onboarding, theme, developer mode; 5 Sep: chat, COP calculator, LOGIC.md, IATA DB; 6–7 Sep: calculator/overlay polish; 8 Sep: inflight menu lookup, viewer overlay, printer; v1.4.0 = 10 Sep, the first onboarding SemVer; v1.4.24 = 11 Sep; v1.4.26 and v1.4.28–29 = 13 Sep; the v1.5.0–1.5.4 run = 14 Sep; v1.5.5–1.5.9 = 15 Sep) and the in-between versions (v1.1.x–v1.3.12, v1.4.9/15/19/23) were placed on that timeline to match; **all 113 entries now carry `d`** (v1.0.0 renders “Sept 2026”). dev-ui E6 now requires a date on every version, and the oldest-header assert pins the inception date. Tests: 11 suites / 1381 asserts / 0 fail; sw v165.

**v1.35.0 hotfix 5 (owner spec, no version bump — still 1.35.0, sw v166):** (1) *Changelog date labels follow the owner ladder, frozen at open:* **Just now** (< 2 min) / **Today** (same calendar day) / **Yesterday** / **X days ago** (2–6) / **Last week** (7–13) / **X weeks ago** (14–30) / short month + day (“Oct 14”, older this year) / short month + year (“Mar 2025”, previous years). Calendar-day math, never clock math; no times, hours or minutes anywhere. Labels compute against `clNow` — stamped the moment `openChangelog` runs — so they never switch while the sheet is on screen (filter/sort re-renders reuse it; a fresh open restamps). Date-only `d` anchors to local midnight, so “Just now” only fires for time-carrying entries (none yet — future-proofing). (2) *Settings polish:* a gold-ringed avatar circle with the crew’s initial beside the profile name (`#settings-avatar`, painted wherever the profile name is); “Usually replies within a few hours” under Chat with the developer; the tour row reads “See everything the app can do in 90 seconds” with a lucide **play** icon (was sparkles); the changelog row reads “New features, fixes, and improvements”; a thin `ca-hairline` divider between the Feature tour and Changelog rows; the Telegram button uses lucide **send** (was message-square — Lucide has no Telegram glyph and the label says Telegram anyway); the Recommended badge is a **green pill with a filled green star** (`.ca-badge-green` — the ★ text char is gone, the star is an icon rendered by `syncCalcUiPills`; green reads “safe choice” without fighting the gold accent); the Smart automation description is the owner copy (“Just enter your flight number and date — sector time, stations, and arrival/departure times fill in automatically.”); and a “CrewAssist vX” footer (`#settings-semver`) closes the sheet. Tests: dev-ui +23 (ladder unit asserts against crafted instants incl. the 2-minute boundary, frozen-label render via `clNow`, and the settings block); 11 suites / 1404 asserts / 0 fail; sw v166.

**v1.35.0 hotfix 6 (owner orders, no version bump — still 1.35.0, sw v167):** (1) *Version-bump ruling (standing, 2026-10-03):* default = bump both stamps; “do not bump version” freezes `APP_VERSION` while `CACHE_NAME` still bumps (see the SemVer rule). (2) *Changelog scroll-to-top:* `#ca-cl-top` — the chat jump-to-latest button’s exact twin (gold gradient circle, 40px, `ca-hit`, fade + scale) with a chevron-**up**, floating bottom-right above the safe area; visible only while `#ca-changelog-scroll` sits past 48px, tap smooth-scrolls to the top (`scrollTo({ top: 0, behavior: 'smooth' })`, `clPaintTopBtn` mirrors `updateScrollBottomBtn`); every re-render (open / filter / sort) resets the list to its top, like the print preview on view change. (3) *Learn the app un-split:* the hotfix-5 divider between Feature tour and Changelog is removed — they are two rows of ONE section and the divider read as a section split. (4) `_screenshots/` created and GITIGNORED — the owner’s drop zone for phone screenshots so the agent can view the app with the image reader; never commit its contents. Tests: dev-ui 304 (+6 top-button asserts, −1 divider); 11 suites / 1409 asserts / 0 fail; sw v167.

**v1.36.0 (owner bug report: airplane-mode boots broken — version bump per the default ruling; sw v168):** *The report:* on BOTH the owner's phones (iPhone 11 + Honor Magic V3, profiles existing, NOT fresh installs) airplane-mode launch shows the onboarding screen "bugged out visually". *Diagnosis (from OCR of the owner's two `_screenshots/` uploads — the agent has NO vision; see that folder's README for the GitHub-upload → `git fetch` → RapidOCR pipeline):* the arch SVG rendered at its default 300×150 box (a ~180px gap between the tagline and "Welcome aboard.") — **Tailwind was not applied**. Git archaeology: (a) the Tailwind runtime (`cdn.tailwindcss.com`, the app styles EVERYTHING through it at runtime) only joined the SW precache in **v1.26.0 (2026-09-26)** — a phone whose SW predates that serves an unstyled shell offline; (b) **lucide (`unpkg.com/lucide@latest`) and Google Fonts were never cached by any version** (absent from `CDN_ASSETS`, and the cache-first branch did `cache.match(req) || fetch(req)` with **no runtime `cache.put`** — so icons and fonts are missing offline on EVERY phone); (c) install-time CDN failures were silently swallowed (`.catch(() => null)`). *The onboarding half:* the profile key (`crewAssist.profile`) and the gate (`name && gender && rank`) are identical across every build since inception — no cached build can misread a profile, and `showOnboarding()` prefills the name field when one exists; the Honor screenshot shows the EMPTY placeholder, i.e. that session read `appProfile` as null — a storage-layer issue (most likely profile created in the browser tab, not the installed-PWA partition), NOT code. Two questions were put to the owner: what airplane-OFF boots to, and the version string under Let's Go offline. *Fixes:* (1) sw v168 — `CDN_ASSETS` now includes lucide + the Google Fonts CSS; `addWithRetry` (3 tries, 800ms backoff) replaces the silent swallow; `RUNTIME_CACHE_HOSTS` backfills first-party + fonts.gstatic/unpkg/cdnjs/tailwind responses (incl. opaque) into the cache as they are used online. (2) `shellGuard()` + `#ca-shell-fail` — if Tailwind is missing at boot, an honest inline-styled repair screen ("Your data is safe. Connect once and reopen — it repairs itself") replaces the broken render; offline it shows immediately and auto-reloads on `online`, online it gives a slow CDN 6s before showing. (3) `APP_CHANGELOG` v1.36.0: two fix pointers (≤12 words each — v1.36.0 is fixes-only, so the summary card correctly renders ZERO feature pills). *Phone self-heal:* opening the app ONCE online after this ships installs v168 (retried precache) and the next airplane-mode launch renders correctly — or shows the honest repair screen if the network blocks the CDNs. Tests: offline-menus +7 hardening asserts (lucide/fonts precached, retry helper, runtime host list, opaque caching, shell guard) and stamps moved to v168/1.36.0; dev-ui 296 — the summary-card pills + missed-count asserts are now **derived from the source changelog** instead of hardcoding one release's items (fixes-only release ⇒ zero pills is correct); menu-extraction stamps. Tests: 11 suites / 1408 asserts / 0 fail; sw v168.

**v1.36.0 follow-up (owner answer, same day):** the owner completed the cut-off report — with airplane mode OFF and data on, the app boots into the **chat screen normally**. That closes the diagnosis: the installed PWA's storage HAS the profile, so the offline launch ran a document that never read it. Verified by archaeology: every committed build since the Sept 4 scaffold (`c1dce9a`) reads `crewAssist.profile` FIRST in `initData`, gates identically, and ships `#onboarding-view` hidden by default — so no committed build can show a prefill-less onboarding over an existing profile. The offline shells on both phones are therefore ancient (likely dev-era/intermediate deploys from before the SW update flow was reliable), and their SWs stayed frozen because SW updates only happen when the app is opened ONLINE — crew life is mostly-offline usage, and the staleness was invisible online (network-first always served the fresh build). Moot in practice: **the next online open installs v168 regardless of how ancient the active SW is** (registration updates are browser-managed; `updateViaCache: 'none'`; the install now retries), which deletes the ancient cache — offline becomes the current build, styled, with the profile. Owner recovery procedure: open on WiFi → confirm Settings footer reads `CrewAssist v1.36.0` → wait ~30s → close fully → airplane-mode test (expect: chat, styled, data intact). Two fallback diagnostics if anything still misbehaves: the gold `#ca-shell-fail` repair screen means the network is blocking a CDN (reconnect once heals it); a STILL-onboarding offline boot means the SW update itself failed — screenshot the `v1.36.x`-or-older string under Let's Go (OCR route) to identify the stuck build.

**v1.42.0 hotfix round (owner orders 2026-10-08 round 7, same day; NO version bump per the standing no-bump ruling — APP_VERSION stays 1.42.0, cache → v201, the 1.42.0 changelog entry EXTENDED to six items):** seven sequential hotfixes. **(1) Tour skip:** a full backup restored through the WELCOME door now sets `crewAssist.tourDone='1'` before the reload (`restoreFullBackup(payload, opts)` — `opts.fromOnboarding` only) — a restoring user is not a new user, so `maybeOfferTour` (gated on `tourDone`/`tourOffered`) stays silent; the Settings door never touches the flag. **(2) Square login door:** first attempt used `aspect-square`, which CANNOT derive a flex item's main-axis width from a stretched cross-axis height (Chromium rendered the door 20×52) — final: explicit `w-[52px] h-[52px] self-center` (52px = the CTA's py-4 + text-sm line height). **(3) The width hotfixes' ROOT CAUSE — recorded so it never recurs:** the app is styled by the COMMITTED compiled `./tw.css` (repo root, loaded via `<link>`; hotfix 18's shell guard), NOT the CDN. Round 6 added `w-32`/`w-[3.5rem]` WITHOUT rebuilding it, so the new utilities silently did nothing — that is why Edit/Replay/View/Telegram "still weren't the same width" and the door wasn't square. **RULE: any new Tailwind utility class requires rebuilding the production stylesheet: `tests/smoke/node_modules/.bin/tailwindcss -c tw.config.cjs -o tw.css --minify` from the repo root** (grep tip: escaped arbitrary classes appear in tw.css as `.w-\[52px\]` — match with `grep -oF 'w-\[52px\]'`). Smoke now guards this with REAL-GEOMETRY pins: getBoundingClientRect squareness for the door and equal widths for the four `w-32` buttons — a stale stylesheet can never pass again. **(4)** Button + confirm copy read "Reset rates to default" (singular). **(5)** Destructive buttons run Clear All Data's red: `btn-dev-reset` and `btn-clear-menus` (amber → `text-red-600 dark:text-[#ff4c4c] border-red-500/50 dark:border-[#ff4c4c]/50`). **(6)** Dev cluster order is now Save rates → Export rates.json → Reset rates to default. **(7)** `btn-dev-export` label "Export rates.json", restyled to the Export-backup gold (was ca-inset gray). Changelog additions (fix): "Restoring from the welcome screen skips the tour." / "Destructive buttons run red; the login door squares up." / "Dev Export ships rates.json, gold like Export backup." — plus the existing "Reset rates moves below Save rates" pointer corrected to "moves into the Developer section". Tests: dev-ui 439 (gold export + label pin, swap order pin, red pins, square pin, clear-menus red pin), dev-rates confirm-wording pin, earnings-archive 291 (tourDone set by the welcome door, untouched by the Settings door), smoke 34 (square + equal-width geometry, tour retired after the journey). Suite 12/12.

**v1.43.0 (owner orders 2026-10-08 round 8 — the destination overlay + roster sharing; sw v202):** two calendar features, fully owner-specced. **(1) The destination overlay** (`#ca-rc-tag`, lucide `tag`, sits between Next and Close, `p-3` like the other header icons; fills `bg-sia-gold text-black` + `aria-pressed` while on). The grid's markup ALWAYS carries the marks; CSS gates them (`#ca-rc-grid.rc-tags-on .ca-rc-tag/.ca-rc-badge` → display + a .18s pop-in; badges stagger .05s) — so toggling is one class flip and month glides re-render marks for free. Marks come from `rcMarksForMonth(ym)`: `rcTripsAll()` flattens the never-pruned allFlights store, normalizes legs to the calculator chain shape ({dep, arr, depDate: stdYmd, arrDate: staYmd, stdHm: std, staHm: sta, ftHm: ft}), sorts, and runs `rosterChainTrips` (same-day back-to-back turns merge; the >6h ground threshold classifies Layover). Per trip: an OUTBOUND tag on the first sector's depDate (orange `ca-rc-tag-lo` when a true Layover, else green `ca-rc-tag-ta` — a sub-6h overnight turn is a Turnover and stays green), a HOMECOMING tag on the final SIN landing day (green, naming the station it came from; skipped when the trip is one day), and an ARRIVAL BADGE digit on the landing day: `rcArrivalBand` — 0000H→3 (midnight rides with the evening), 0001–1200→1, 1201–1800→2, 1801–2359→3. Tags: Space Mono 8px bold, rotated −14° at the top-right of the day pill; badges: dark chip (night: black/74 + hairline) at the bottom-right; both `pointer-events:none` (a tagged day still selects) and never over the centered number. Day theme runs saturated fills; night theme semi-transparent + hairline borders. **State machine (owner spec, deliberately NOT persisted — the spec's “persists across sessions” line loses to its own emphatic close/dismiss rules; flagged to the owner):** open → default (expanded, overlay off, tag grey, camera hidden); collapse → `rcTagsOn=false` + both buttons hidden; expand → default; close → reset. The CAMERA (`#ca-rc-shot`, lucide `camera`) is visible ONLY while expanded AND overlay-on. **(2) The capture** (`captureRosterShot`): shutter flash (`.ca-rc-flash` keyframe) → `buildRosterShotCanvas()` draws the month title (centered), weekday labels (`#ca-rc-dow`), every pill (computed bg + border-radius via `roundRect`), numbers, today bar, and the tags/badges (tags drawn rotated) onto a canvas at `devicePixelRatio`, background `#F6F1E8` light / `#080B14` dark — faithful by construction because it walks the live DOM's rects and computed styles; fonts resolve after `document.fonts.ready`. `rosterShotDeliver`: `navigator.canShare({files})` → native share sheet, else anchor download; filename `CrewAssist-Roster-<FullMonth>-<Year>.png` (CA_MONTHS); toast "Roster screenshot saved" both ways; `window.__rcLastShot` is a test hook. jsdom guard: rects are 0 without a layout engine → `buildRosterShotCanvas` returns null and the capture no-ops. NOTE: overlay marks come from the PDF-roster store only — chat-added upcoming flights have no station data and earn no tags. **Tests:** roster-calendar +43 (band boundary pins incl. 00:00→3, marks per trip kind incl. the sub-6h overnight, header state machine, CSS-gate + DOM marks, day-tap passthrough, fold/close/reset, filename, jsdom guard, flash, download vs share paths with stubbed createElement/navigator), smoke 41 (real-browser: overlay off/on visibility via getComputedStyle, SYD orange/green + band 2 + KUL, a REAL PNG capture asserted by name+size, collapse rules, close). dev-ui's count moved 439→428 — its changelog block is DYNAMIC (per-pointer asserts on the current release's items; 6 items → 2). Suite 12/12 (1,921 asserts; the 1,941 first written here was a hand-miscount). **Also:** the sandbox drifted a FOURTH time mid-round (stale base + node_modules wiped) — fetch-and-reset to 816fb9b + reinstalls, no loss.

**v1.43.0 hotfix (owner orders 2026-10-08 round 9 — button order, the gold toggle, one tag per destination; no version bump — still 1.43.0, sw v203):** three orders on the round-8 calendar. **(1) BUTTON ORDER:** `#ca-rc-tag` moved out of the right cluster to the LEFT cluster, immediately right of the fold chevron, with `#ca-rc-shot` immediately right of the tag — left cluster = month label (press-hold jump), fold, tag, camera; right = prev, next, close (the owner found the pair crowded against the nav arrows). **(2) THE GOLD BUG, root-caused:** the tag toggle looked un-highlighted on the owner's phone although the code was right — a touch tap LATCHES `:hover` on mobile, and Tailwind's `hover:bg-black/5 dark:hover:bg-white/5` (specificity 0,2,0 — class + pseudo) outranks the toggled `bg-sia-gold` utility (0,1,0); a Chromium probe proved the classes AND the computed gold fill landed at rest, so sticky hover was the only differentiator. FIX: `rcTagsSync` toggles a single `ca-rc-tagon` class (no bg-/text- utilities), and CSS `#ca-rc-tag.ca-rc-tagon { background: #C9A227; color: #0B0E14; }` — ID specificity beats any hover wash. STANDING RULE for this repo: an active/selected state on a tappable control is an ID-level rule, never a toggled bg-* utility (touch latches hover). **(3) ONE TAG PER DESTINATION:** `rcMarksForMonth` v2 — every DISTINCT non-SIN stop is tagged exactly ONCE, on the day the crew first ARRIVES there: SIN-SYD-SIN gets one SYD on the outbound day and the homecoming day keeps only its arrival badge (no tag); an overnight-outbound trip tags on the downrange ARRIVAL day (15 Oct dep → the tag lands on the 16th beside its badge, the 15th goes bare); multi-sector same-day runs name every stop — SIN-KUL-PEN-SIN draws KUL and PEN on the one day, first tag top-right (`slot-a`), second top-left (`.ca-rc-tag.slot-b { left:-3px; right:auto; }`), same −14° tilt. Color still by trip type (true Layover → orange `lo`, else green `ta`). Marks shape per day is now `{ tags: [{c,k}…] (max 2), badge }` (was `{tag, tagKind, badge}`), and days with neither drop out of the map entirely. ALSO: `rosterChainTrips`' Layover/Turnaround type computation moved ABOVE the `if (!trip.ok) return trip;` bail — 3-sector trips previously froze as the default 'Layover' (priced 2/4-sector values unchanged) — and `buildRosterShotCanvas` iterates `day.querySelectorAll('.ca-rc-tag')` so both slots render in captures. **Tests:** roster-calendar 269 (+5: the 3-sector same-day seed legs 131/132/133 with an Oct-18 duty day, once-each tags incl. the bare homecoming day and the bare overnight-out day, HKT on the 16th, slot-a/slot-b DOM pins, the ca-rc-tagon class pin, the fold→tag→shot order); dev-ui 434 (the dynamic changelog block picked up the two new pointers); smoke 43 (+2: the chevron-order check and the gold fill as a COMPUTED style rgb(201, 162, 39) — immune to the sticky hover; the tags-render pin rewritten to the v2 rules). Suite 12/12 (1,932 asserts). Changelog: the 1.43.0 entry extended to 4 pointers — the two round-8 features plus 'One tag per destination; multi-sector trips name every stop.' and 'The tag toggle sits by the chevron and stays gold.'

**v1.43.0 hotfix round 10 (owner orders 2026-10-08 — per-stop tag colors, the capture as-is, touch tap states; no version bump — still 1.43.0, sw v204):** the owner's items 1–3 (button order, the gold toggle, one tag per destination) had shipped in round 9; this round built the three new orders. **(1) PER-STOP TAG COLORS:** `rcMarksForMonth` colors each stop by the ground time AT that station (the same 6h threshold as the chain classifier) instead of the trip's overall type — SIN-JNB-CPT-JNB-SIN wears ORANGE at JNB (the first visit lays over) and GREEN at CPT (a quick turn) on the SAME trip; a repeated station keeps its first visit's color and never re-tags. Every existing color pin held (the sub-6h HKT overnight stays green, SYD orange, same-day stop runs green). **(2) THE CAPTURE IS AS-IS (owner order: no redrawn grid; only the header may be drawn):** the round-8 hand-drawn grid is gone. `rosterShotPaintGrid` is a GENERIC walker with zero calendar-specific rules: every visible element's computed box (fill, border, radius, opacity), rotations about the element's center (the angle read off the computed transform matrix), and every text run at its live `Range`-rect position (`document.createRange()` over text nodes — pixel-true placement, and canvas fillText keeps the page's real webfonts, unlike an SVG image context). The deliver paths (share/download, filename, toast, `window.__rcLastShot`) are unchanged; the hook now also carries the blob. **DEAD END, do not retry: an SVG foreignObject rasterizer (cloned DOM with inlined computed styles) rasterizes beautifully but Chromium TAINTS any canvas that drew an SVG containing a foreignObject — `toBlob` refuses to export. Proven minimal in this rig: a bare `<foreignObject><div>hi</div></foreignObject>` already taints.** **(3) TOUCH TAP STATES:** `tw.config.cjs` sets `future: { hoverOnlyWhenSupported: true }` and tw.css was rebuilt — every hover utility now compiles behind `@media (hover:hover) and (pointer:fine)`, so a finger tap (which latches `:hover` on touch) can never leave a wash behind after release. Class-based states — the gold `ca-rc-tagon` toggle, the two-tap confirms — are untouched by design; the inline <style> hover rules were already gated (the v1.39-era "mouse-only luxury" block). **Tests:** roster-calendar 277 (+8: the SIN-JNB-CPT-JNB-SIN seed with per-stop pins — JNB lo on the 20th, CPT ta on the 21st, no JNB re-tag on the second visit, badge-1 homecoming — plus a jsdom no-deliver pin and the blob hook), dev-ui 447 (+13: three new changelog pointers ×3; a brace-walking scanner asserting EVERY hover utility in tw.css sits inside a (hover:hover) media block; the inline-gate pin; the hotfix-19 pin now bounds its `</style>` search before the first `<script`, because the painter's JS strings confused the naive lastIndexOf), smoke 46 (+3: the capture decoded via `createImageBitmap` with a distinct-color floor proving the grid rasterized; a REAL finger tap on the calendar button settles back to its resting color while the toggle's gold survives the release; the What's New close check became honest). **Rig lessons:** the smoke viewport now runs `hasTouch: true` (Chrome then natively reports (hover:none)/(pointer:coarse) — puppeteer's `emulateMediaFeatures` rejects 'hover'); any reload re-offers What's New (the v1.35.0 ruling: closing never counts as seen — corrected in round 11; it is the design, not a restore artifact), and its backdrop had been silently covering the app for every check after it — all older checks were evaluate-driven and never noticed; and the chat-busy rest (hotfix 12) rightly refuses the calendar button while the welcome chat types, so the rig waits for chat idle before finger-tapping. Suite 12/12 (1,953 asserts). Changelog: the 1.43.0 entry carries 7 pointers.

**v1.43.0 hotfix round 11 (owner orders 2026-10-08 — larger marks, the badge tucked inward, animated show/hide, the What's New ruling confirmed; no version bump — still 1.43.0, sw v205):** **(0) WHAT'S NEW re-offer — CONFIRMED AS-IS (owner ruling):** the app already behaves as ordered (the v1.35.0 ruling): What's New returns on EVERY launch until the crew ticks "Do not show again" — merely closing never stamps anything (`hideWhatsNew`/`wnSeen` write only via the checkbox, per version). No app change; the smoke pins the trio (re-offered after a reload, a plain close re-offers again on the next boot, checkbox + close stays quiet forever). **(1) LARGER MARKS:** the IATA tag grows 8→9px (line-height 11→12px, side padding 4.5→5px); the arrival badge 8.5→9.5px (chip 12→14px tall, padding 3→4px) and shifts inward from the pill's corner (bottom/right 2/1px → 4/4px, clear of the number). The capture painter reads computed styles, so captures grow with them for free. **(2) ANIMATED SHOW/HIDE (the standing ruling, now applied to the round-9 buttons):** `rcBtnAnimate(el, show)` pops a button in (.16s, scale .55→1) on arrival and pops it out (.15s) before `hidden` lands at +170ms; rapid toggles cancel pending timers, a rescue mid-pop-out clears the out class and pops back in, and a no-op sync never replays (guards on the element's current state). `rcTagsSync` drives the tag and camera buttons through it. **(3) MARKS POP OUT:** turning the overlay off no longer blinks the marks away — the grid wears a transient `rc-tags-out` class for 260ms whose CSS keeps the marks displayed under reverse pop animations (`ca-rc-popout`/`ca-rc-popbout`, the badge keeps its .05s stagger); re-toggling on cancels the timer and clears the class. **Tests:** roster-calendar 282 (+5: the out-animation block — source pin, out classes land before display:none, cleanup + hidden landing, the triple-tap rescue — with the hide waits stretched for the 170ms exit), dev-ui 456 (+9: three new changelog pointers ×3), smoke 51 (+5: the What's New ruling trio, the larger-marks computed pin, the real-browser pop-out pin reading animationName off the mark and the camera). **Rig lesson:** the one-time install hint (a tab user with saved earnings — the restored phone qualifies) fires on a quiet moment, and the ruling pins' extra reloads handed it one; the stage-clear now dismisses an app-dialog too. **The sandbox drifted a FIFTH time mid-round — the worst yet: local git reset to the branch base 14409f3 AND both node_modules wiped, though the working tree (round-11 edits included) survived intact. Recovery: fetch + `git reset origin/<branch>` (mixed — re-points HEAD to the pushed commit, keeps the worktree) + reinstalls; zero loss, and the recipe is now proven for the reset-flavor of drift.** Suite 12/12 (1,967 asserts). Changelog: the 1.43.0 entry carries 10 pointers.

**v1.43.0 hotfix round 12 (owner orders 2026-10-08 — the station-list swap + the calendar clash hotfix; no version bump — still 1.43.0, sw v206):** **(1) THE STATION LIST SWAP:** the calculator's 574-entry `airports` list is now the owner's curated 139-station SQ/Scoot network — every station the crew can actually be rostered to. KNO added on the owner's correction; PNH kept alongside KTI because Pochentong was Phnom Penh's IATA until Techo opened 9 Sep 2025 (pre-swap rosters still speak PNH; KTI = Techo verified, and WSI = Western Sydney/Nancy-Bird Walton verified — SQ's daily A350 starts 23 Nov 2026). **(2) AIRPORT_TZ REBUILT:** 139 keys, full coverage, zero orphans — the zone map now covers exactly the list (Indonesia splits WIB `Asia/Jakarta` vs WITA `Asia/Makassar`; KH `Asia/Phnom_Penh`, BN `Asia/Brunei`, LA `Asia/Vientiane`, LGW/LHR/MAN `Europe/London`, DRW `Australia/Darwin`, WSI/SYD `Australia/Sydney`). **(3) BN/LA JOIN THE REGION MAP:** `countryToRegion` maps Brunei and Laos to "Southeast Asia" — SEA rates apply. **(4) CALENDAR CLASH HOTFIX:** the IATA tag grows 9→10px (line-height 12→13px) and the arrival badge to a 15px chip (font 9.5→10.5px, line-height 15px) tucked inward to bottom/right 6px — and the enlarged pair now owns disjoint bands (the tag the top, the badge the bottom, per the CSS contract comment) so they can never intersect on a both-marks day; the contract is pinned in the real browser. Changelog: the 1.43.0 entry grows to 12 pointers (two new fix-pointers, one sentence each). **Tests:** next-flight 123 (+14 — the round-12 block pins the data NODE-SIDE: the list and zone map are regex-extracted from the source and eval'd in node, because the app's top-level `const`s are eval-scoped in the harness and never global; 139 entries, no dupes, KNO/KTI/PNH/WSI present, TZ full coverage + zero orphans, spot zones, plus page-side region/zone/landing-phrase pins for the new stations), dev-ui 462 (+6 — the two new changelog pointers ×3), roster-calendar 282 unchanged, menu-extraction + offline-menus stamp pins → v206. Smoke 52 (+1: the intersection pin on a both-marks day — with the lesson that the badge is the ARRIVAL BAND, not a flight count: the 11:45 homecoming wears band 1, and the pin's first draft expecting '2' failed while the geometry was innocent). **Rig lessons:** (a) after a workspace restore the smoke needs `node tests/smoke/build-app.js` FIRST — `app.html` + `tw.css` are generated, not persisted, and the server 404s 'nope' without them; (b) the shell kills long piped foreground runs mid-suite (run-all twice died silently ~3–4 min in, log truncated mid-list) — run the suites singly and sum; (c) the next-flight structural block needs its own `const fs = require('fs')` (the harness exports APP but not fs — the missing import crashed the suite before its summary line). Suite 12/12 (1,987 asserts). Smoke 52/52, zero console errors.

**v1.43.0 hotfix round 13 (owner orders 2026-10-08 — the KUL wake-up exception; no version bump — still 1.43.0, sw v207):** the next-flight card's suggested alarm is no longer one-size-fits-all. A KUL-bound outbound (the part-one card whose outbound ends at KUL — `station === 'KUL'`) wakes at **STD − 3h30m** with the report line at **STD − 1h30m**; every other station keeps the original chain (alarm −4h, report −2h — the NRT pins hold it). The display window still opens 12h before the alarm it shows, so KUL's window opens 15.5h before STD instead of the old flat 16h (the lead constants drive both lines and the window edge together — no way for them to drift apart). KUL shares the SGT clock, so no conversion is involved either way. Changelog: the 1.43.0 entry grows to 13 pointers ("The KUL wake-up and report lines sit closer to departure."). **Tests:** next-flight 127 (+4: the KUL block — alarm 0455H off an 0825 STD, report line 0655H, and the window pin: hidden at 15h45m out, where an NRT card at the same offset would already show, proving the window follows the alarm it displays), dev-ui 465 (+3: the new pointer ×3), menu-extraction + offline-menus stamp pins → v207, smoke changelog pin → 13 items (new,new + fix×11) and sw v207. Suite 12/12 (1,994 asserts). Smoke 52/52, zero console errors.

**v1.43.0 hotfix round 14 (owner orders 2026-10-08 — the owner's own AIRPORT_TZ recode, adopted; no version bump — still 1.43.0, sw v208):** the owner rewrote the timezone section from scratch and the review found it was ~95% the app's own algorithm already (the v1.38.0 A2 helpers — `airportZone`, two-pass DST `zonedWallToUtcMs`, `landingSgtPhrase` — matched line for line, an independent re-derivation). **Adopted:** (1) the map regrouped by region, one key per line, with East Malaysia on its own geographically true zones (BKI/KCH/MYY/SBW → `Asia/Kuching` — same fixed UTC+8 clock as Kuala Lumpur, zero behavioral change); (2) `validateAirportTimezones()` + the load-time `TZ_VALIDATION` — a runtime guard the app lacked (coverage lived only in the test suite): never throws, `console.error`s missing entries (the smoke's zero-console-error rule turns any future miss into a caught failure) and only warns on orphans (historical codes may linger); (3) the console debug pair `debugAirportTimezone`/`debugSgtConversion` (inert unless called); (4) the explicit `!zone` guard in `zonedWallToUtcMs`. **Two fixes made during adoption:** (a) the recode had DROPPED SUB — restored to `Asia/Jakarta` (Surabaya keeps WIB: all of Java runs +7, verified against time.now + savvytime before touching it — the instinct to move it to WITA for "East Java" is WRONG); (b) the single-digit-hour wart, pre-existing in both versions: the guard regex admits `'8:25'` but `Date.parse` rejects `'T8:25:00Z'` → NaN — now padded before parsing. Changelog: the 1.43.0 entry grows to 14 pointers ("Time zones now self-check at boot; East Malaysia zones read true."). **Tests:** next-flight 134 (+7: SUB keeps WIB, the Kuching four, the validator exists page-side + returns valid with zero missing/orphans + never throws, `TZ_VALIDATION` stays eval-scoped, the single-digit hour pads and equals its padded twin, and a BKI landing phrase stays empty — Kuching shares the SGT clock), dev-ui 468 (+3: the new pointer ×3), menu-extraction + offline-menus stamp pins → v208, smoke changelog pin → 14 items. Suite 12/12 (2,004 asserts). Smoke 52/52, zero console errors — which is itself proof the real-browser boot ran the validator clean.

**v1.43.0 hotfix round 15 (owner orders 2026-10-08 — the marks pop ONCE, and the swipe respects the data edges; no version bump — still 1.43.0, sw v209):** **(1) THE ONE-SHOT POP-IN:** the marks' pop-in animation moved off the persistent `rc-tags-on` class onto a transient `rc-tags-pop` — added only on a true off→on transition (the toggle tap, or a rescue mid-pop-out), removed by a 400ms timer, and stripped at once by every `rcPaintGrid` repaint. So: toggling the overlay on pops the marks in (unchanged, as ordered), but an arrow month change or a swipe month change — which re-renders the grid as fresh DOM — renders its marks already settled, never replaying the pop; re-syncs (collapse/expand paths that don't transition) never re-pop either. **(2) THE EDGE-DEAD SWIPE:** the arrows gray out and refuse at the data edges, but a HOLD-then-swipe (the natural phone gesture: press, rest a beat, drag — exactly the 140ms park of hotfix 18) still rubber-banded the cloned void month in and snapped back, reading as "you can swipe there." The parked track's intent branch now checks the data edge first and DISARMS at once — `rcDragStop(true)` restores the grid, no `ca-rc-drag`, no track, nothing moves — while the window listeners stay live so a finger that swings toward a real month still engages normally. The commit guard (`has`) and the flick threshold were already edge-safe; the disarm closes the last moving path. Changelog: the 1.43.0 entry grows to 16 pointers ("Marks pop in once; month changes never replay the pop." + "The swipe respects the data edges exactly like the arrows."). **Tests:** roster-calendar 297 (+15 in a standalone Oct+Nov block: the source pin — pop rides `rc-tags-pop`, never `rc-tags-on`; fresh-transition pops then the class leaves after its window; an ARROW month change keeps the overlay lit with the November mark settled; a FLICK month change never re-pops; the hold-then-swipe toward the missing month disarms with no drag/track and the release changes nothing at BOTH edges; the control — the same gesture toward a real month still glides; the fast race — a repaint mid-window strips the pop at once; the rescue re-pops), dev-ui 474 (+6: two new pointers ×3), smoke 57 (+5, real browser: settled marks read `animationName: none` after the window; a fresh toggle reads `ca-rc-pop`; the arrow lands on November with the fresh mark settled; a REAL mouse hold-then-swipe toward the missing December disarms with no drag and no track; the release keeps November) — the smoke fixture now carries a November duty so the calendar has an edge to respect. Suite 12/12 (2,025 asserts). Smoke 57/57, zero console errors.

**v1.43.0 hotfix round 16 (owner orders 2026-10-08 — the owner's cleaned station-data section adopted + the calendar header one-row hotfix; sw v210):** **(1) THE ADOPTION:** the owner recoded the whole station-data section ultra-vertical — the 139-entry `airports` list (one station per line, region header comments), the `AIRPORT_TZ` map (one key per line, `};` at column 0), and all seven helpers (`airportZone`, `zonedWallToUtcMs`, `landingSgtPhrase`, `validateAirportTimezones`, `TZ_VALIDATION`, `debugAirportTimezone`, `debugSgtConversion`) at column 0 with `// ====` banner comments — spliced in wholesale, replacing the old block. Data verified equivalent before adoption: the list diffed zero field/order deltas, the map diffed 139 identical keys with zero value deltas (SUB stays Asia/Jakarta — all Java runs WIB; never "fix" it to Makassar), and the one behavior delta is a real fix: `landingSgtPhrase` pads single-digit hours before the same-clock comparison, so a KUL arrival seeded `8:25` now correctly stays silent (the old unpadded compare missed the SGT match and printed a bogus "08:25 SGT" line). **HARNESS NOTE (critical):** the map's closer moved to column 0, so `next-flight.test.js`'s old 8-space-anchored `tzSrc` regex over-captured past the map — it is now indentation-agnostic (`/const AIRPORT_TZ = {[\s\S]*?\n\s*};/`) and carries a round-16 comment; two new pins ride it (KUL `8:25` → `''`, KTI `8:25` → `09:25 SGT`). **(2) THE HEADER ONE-ROW HOTFIX (owner report, iPhone 11):** with the tag overlay on, "Oct 2026" wrapped onto two rows on the owner's iPhone 11 (fine on the wider Honor Magic V3). Diagnosis: the header chrome (jump+month+fold left, tag+camera beside it, prev+next+close right) sums past a narrow row, and Safari — correctly refusing to shrink the padded buttons below their content+padding — dumps the whole squeeze onto the only breakable item, the two-word month label (zoomed-display iPhone 11 runs an effective ~375px viewport; headless Chromium shrank the buttons instead, which is why the wrap never reproduced there). Fix, two rules in the calendar CSS: `#ca-rc-month { white-space: nowrap; }` (one row is the contract, whatever the width) and a `@media (max-width: 400px)` trim — fold p-2.5→6px, tag/shot/prev/next p-3→8px, close p-3.5→10px — so the label keeps its room on narrow phones; the `.ca-hit` halo (inset −5px) keeps every touch target comfortably ≥44px, and wide screens never enter the media query. Changelog: the 1.43.0 entry grows to 18 pointers ("Landing conversions compare clocks with padded hours." + "The calendar month never wraps to two rows."). **Tests:** next-flight 136 (regex relaxation + the two `8:25` pins), roster-calendar 299 (+2: the nowrap/media-query source pin and the computed `whiteSpace: nowrap`), smoke 59 (+2: at 390px with the overlay on the month stays one row and the header groups never collide; at 375px — the zoomed-iPhone class — the trim engages and it still holds), dev-ui 480 (+6 auto, the changelog-pointer pins scale with the entry). Suite 12/12 (2,035 asserts). Smoke 59/59, zero console errors.

**v1.43.0 hotfix round 17 (owner orders 2026-10-10 — the suggested alarm retires when it rings, and multi-sector days suggest it once; sw v211):** **(1) THE RUNG ALARM:** the next-flight card's suggested-alarm block used to persist from its 12h-before opening until DEPARTURE — so at 05:41 for a 0540H alarm it still suggested a wake-up that had already happened. The window now closes at the ALARM ITSELF (`now < depMs - alarmLead`, replacing `now < depMs`); the reporting sub-line rides the block it belongs to. The card only re-rendered on leg landings and chat events, so a rung alarm would have lingered on screen until the next event — the new `nfAlarmSweep()` joins the 60s `caRealtimeSweep` tick and re-renders whenever the card's alarm block disagrees with the clock. The mechanism: `renderNextFlightCard` stamps `data-alarm-from`/`data-alarm-until` (the window edges, present-or-not, only when the alarm's preconditions hold) on the card element, and the sweep compares those stamps against `Date.now()` versus the block's presence (the block now carries the `ca-nf-alarm` class). This retires a rung alarm AND reveals one whose window opens mid-session — both edges, same check. **(2) THE MULTI-SECTOR DAY:** on a turnaround day with two SIN departures (e.g. SIN-KUL-SIN morning, SIN-KUL evening), the card's anchor moves to the evening sector once the morning legs land — part1 true again, alarm window open, so the card suggested a SECOND wake-up for a crew already awake. New gate `nfAlarmDayFirst(first)`: the alarm renders only for the day's FIRST departure — any earlier leg the same `ymd` (checked against the never-pruned `crewAssist.allFlights` twin, because the upcoming store forgets legs the moment they land; both stores fill together on roster import) silences it; no clock on record keeps the suggestion. The gate sits beside the existing `part1 && depMs && !inAir` conditions and falls back to showing on any error. Changelog: the 1.43.0 entry grows to 20 pointers ("A rung suggested alarm leaves the next-flight card." + "Multi-sector days suggest one alarm — the first departure."). **Tests:** next-flight 143 (+7 in four round-17 blocks: at 05:44 the 0545H alarm still shows; at 05:40 the card stamps `data-alarm-until` at the alarm instant, and re-freezing the page clock to 05:46 then calling `nfAlarmSweep()` retires the block while the flight card itself stays; a two-turnaround day viewed at 12:00 shows the evening sector with NO suggestion; the same day at 03:00 still suggests 0455H for the morning's first departure — the KUL −3h30m lead), dev-ui 486 (+6: the changelog-pointer pins scale with the entry, and the sweep-cadence source pin now names the alarm sweep), smoke 59 (changelog pin 18→20). Suite 12/12 (2,048 asserts). Smoke 59/59, zero console errors. NOTE: the smoke exercises no alarm pins (its duty sits 2 days out — outside every window); the jsdom pins carry the round with frozen clocks, the round-13 precedent.

**v1.43.0 round 18 (owner-approved audit improvement #1, 2026-10-10 — the refreshIcons() helper; pure refactor, ZERO behaviour change; no version bump, no sw bump, no changelog pointer — user-invisible):** the guarded icon-refresh `try { lucide.createIcons(); } catch (e) {}` was inlined 37 times across script blocks 2 and 3; it is now ONE top-level `refreshIcons()` function declared in block 2 right after the boot-time icon init (which keeps its own LOGGING catch — a first-load failure deserves noise, and it is NOT one of the 37), and all 37 sites call it. Deliberately NOT touched: the boot init's logging catch; the `catch (err)` spelling variant at the former line 12,445 (a 38th near-twin the owner has not approved folding in); the three `if (!caBulkBuild …)` conditional scans; the seven bare unguarded `lucide.createIcons()` calls in the menu-viewer block (pre-existing fragility, unchanged); two comments mentioning the call. Reachability: block 2's top-level function declaration is global before block 3 runs (verified in a fresh vm context), and the jsdom harness's `w.lucide` stub is installed before script eval — identical call semantics both ways; the smoke (CDN blocked) exercised the catch path for real. **One test pin updated:** roster-calendar's justMounted source pin now matches `if (justMounted) { refreshIcons(); }` and additionally pins the helper's presence and ≥37 call sites. Lesson for refactors of this shape: run the substitution BEFORE adding a helper whose body contains the search string, or the substitution eats the helper (it did; caught and fixed in-review — the final diff was re-audited line by line: 37 removals, 44 additions, nothing else). **Tests:** all 12 suites unchanged in count — dev-ui 486, next-flight 143, roster-calendar 299, earnings-archive 291, roster-real 332, roster-pdf 191, tour 130, menu-extraction 48, offline-menus 65, exports 26, dev-rates 25, trip-bar 12 = 2,048 asserts, 0 failed; smoke 59/59, zero page errors, zero console errors. Improvement #2 (loadJsonStore for the three twin loaders) SHIPPED this round — see the entry titled "v1.43.0 round 18, improvement #2".

**v1.43.0 round 18, improvement #2 (owner-approved, 2026-10-10 — the loadJsonStore helper; pure refactor, ZERO behaviour change; no version bump, no sw bump, no changelog pointer):** `loadUpcoming` / `loadAllFlights` / `loadDutyDays` were byte-identical guarded loaders modulo their key constant — `try { JSON.parse(localStorage.getItem(KEY) || '{}'); object-or-{} } catch { {} }`. They now delegate to one shared `loadJsonStore(key, fallback)` declared just above `loadUpcoming` (same script block — the function declaration is global for every caller, page and harness alike). Names, keys, interfaces and fallback semantics unchanged; the helper body is the original expression with the `{}` literals replaced by the `fallback` parameter (all three call sites pass `{}`, so every input class behaves identically: missing/empty value, valid object, ARRAY passthrough quirk, JSON null, non-object JSON, corrupt JSON). **Tests:** roster-real grows a 6-pin corrupt-store spot check (garbage / empty-string / JSON-null seeds → each loader returns {}, the app stands, and a valid store round-trips) — 338 total; every other suite unchanged in count (dev-ui 486, next-flight 143, roster-calendar 299, earnings-archive 291, roster-pdf 191, tour 130, menu-extraction 48, offline-menus 65, exports 26, dev-rates 25, trip-bar 12) = 12/12, 2,054 asserts, 0 failed; smoke 59/59, zero page errors, zero console errors. ** Flake on record (pre-existing, test-side, NOT this change):** dev-ui's bare-command pin (dev-ui.test.js ~813) reads the chat's last bubble immediately after `send()` with no `await idle()` — under load the bot bubble can still be forming, `pick` comes back empty, and `pick[1].click()` crashes the suite. It fired once mid-round; the same code passed 486/486 three times with the change applied and once stashed without it. A future pin-hardening (add `await idle(); await wait()` before the read) is worth the owner's nod — NOT done here (no unrelated changes). Improvement #3 SHIPPED — see the entry titled "v1.43.0 round 18, improvement #3".

**v1.43.0 round 18, improvement #3 (owner-approved, 2026-10-10 — delete the verified-dead importDevRatesFile; zero behaviour change by construction; no version bump, no sw bump, no changelog pointer):** the function (16 lines, FileReader-based rates import into the dev editor) was orphaned in v1.41.0 round 5 — that round removed the dev footer's Import button (`#btn-dev-import` / `dev-file-import`) and its siblings `archImport`/`archImportRead`, but this function survived the sweep. Full re-verification before deletion: plain-name grep across the whole repo (only the declaration + the generated app.html artifact + historical HANDOVER prose), substring/alias forms (`importDev`, `DevRatesFile`), string/indirect forms (`window[...]`, `globalThis`, `self`, eval-string, setTimeout-string), inline event-handler attributes, the app's three real file inputs (all route to backup/roster paths), and window-enumeration reflection — ZERO live references. Deleted exactly lines 8,977–8,992 plus one separator blank (−15 lines); adjacent export block and loadShippedRates untouched. **Tests:** dev-rates 25/25, dev-ui 486/486, then the full battery 12/12 = 2,054 asserts, 0 failed (roster-real 338, next-flight 143, roster-calendar 299, earnings-archive 291, roster-pdf 191, tour 130, menu-extraction 48, offline-menus 65, exports 26, trip-bar 12); smoke 59/59 on the rebuilt artifact (see the flake note — it took reruns).

**TEST-MAINTENANCE TASKS (owner ruling 2026-10-10: fix separately, never inside a refactor round; all are pre-existing/test-side and app-side behaviour is CORRECT in all three).** **STATUS: 1 CLOSED (hardening committed) · 2 OPEN · 3 CLOSED in round 20. One task is still live — item 2.**
1. **[CLOSED — the hardening is committed: the read rides `send()`'s idle waits and the pin waits out the choice click (dev-ui.test.js:816); not fired since round 18] dev-ui.test.js ~813 (bare-command pin):** reads the chat's last bubble immediately after `send()` with no `await idle()` — under load the bot bubble is still forming, `pick` is empty, and `pick[1].click()` crashes the suite. Hardening: `await idle(); await wait(300)` before the read. Fired once this round; passed 486/486 on three subsequent runs with the change and once without.
2. **[CLOSED — round 27, 2026-10-11: owner-approved class assert + bounded colour poll] smoke.js tail (the toggle-is-exempt pin):** ROOT CAUSE DIAGNOSED this round via an instrumented throwaway — the tap flips the `ca-rc-tagon` class instantly, but the button carries Tailwind's `transition-colors` (150ms), so the COMPUTED backgroundColor fades to gold; the pin's fixed `sleep(300)` then string-compares the computed colour, and under main-thread jank (reveal passes, icon scans, capture cleanup all settling) the transition starts late and 300ms is not always enough → false FAIL with the class correctly present. Flake rate rose after the post-drift chromium reinstall (same binary family, tighter timer scheduling); the true-baseline stash experiment failed 2/2 while the identical code passed 2/2 in round 17 — environmental, NOT the round-18 changes. Hardening (when approved): assert the `ca-rc-tagon` CLASS (logical state, immune to transition timing) or poll the colour to settled — do NOT lengthen blindly. **FIXED IN ROUND 27 (owner-approved class + poll, 2026-10-11): the pin now asserts the `ca-rc-tagon` class first, then polls the computed gold to settled inside a bounded, caught 2s waitForFunction — the fixed sleep(300) is gone, a colour timeout reports as a failed check instead of crashing the rig, and the check count goes 61→62 (both labels keep the "hotfix: the toggle is exempt" prefix). Smoke ×5 on the fix: 62/62 ×5, zero failures — on the same app.html bytes (md5 7bd72974…) that fired the flake 3/5 the same morning. TASK CLOSED.**
3. **[CLOSED — round 20, 2026-10-10] smoke.js round-18 fv tap pin (added in improvement #5; diagnosed in round 19 via six instrumented throwaways):** the pin taps `[data-fv-act=today]` after a fixed `sleep(400)` — but while the typing queue is active `html.chat-busy` holds `pointer-events: none` over EVERY `#chat-container button` (hotfix 12, by design; programmatic .click() sails past, real taps do not), so the trusted tap lands on the pointer-dead button and no click reaches the delegated listener. It passed its single round-18 run by queue-timing luck and failed 2/2 in round 19 (fresh sandbox, different typing-queue timing — the welcome sequence types for ~4.8s after boot, and any still-queued bot reply re-arms the lock mid-pin). NOT an app bug: the OLD inline onclick was equally blocked (pointer-events gates the click itself, not the binding). **CHAT-BUSY HALF FIXED (round 19b, owner-approved):** the pin now loops { waitForFunction(not chat-busy, 20s) → sleep(700) → still not busy? break } before the tap — the 700ms stability re-check exists because the queue has not-busy GAPS between messages (a single wait resolves inside a gap and the next message re-arms the lock). **GHOST-SHEET HALF REMAINS — and it is an APP bug, not a test bug (diagnosed to root cause in round 19b, deterministic repro, awaiting the owner's ruling):** smoke section 7 does `openSettings()` → width checks → `closeSettings()`; when the checks finish inside openSettings's 10ms glide window, closeSettings arms `caSheetHideArm` (backdrop → hidden at 300ms) and re-adds `translate-y-full`, then the late `caSheetGlideIn` timer FIRES ANYWAY (no closer cancels it), removes `translate-y-full` (RE-OPENS the sheet) and un-dims the shade — the armed timer then hides the backdrop → **a ghost sheet: open, no backdrop, covering the chat (z-50)**. Repro (real Chromium): one evaluate with `openSettings(); closeSettings();` → after 1.2s the sheet sits OPEN (y=60) with the backdrop HIDDEN; a 200ms-gapped pair closes cleanly. A fast-fingered real user can hit this (gear then immediate backdrop tap/Escape inside 10ms) on EVERY sheet using the delayed-glide pattern — settings, changelog, archive, results, menu viewer, printer. The smoke's fv pin is the only pin that taps into the bare chat area, which is why only it (and occasionally the task-2 toggle, by co-incidence of independent flakes) exposes the ghost. **Proposed fix when approved:** store the glide timeout id on the sheet (`sheet.__caGlideT`) in the opener and `clearTimeout` it in the closer — the exact inversion of hotfix 14's "opens cancel; closes arm" discipline, applied one place per sheet or as a shared `caSheetGlideArm`/`caSheetGlideCancel` pair — plus a jsdom pin (sync open+close → sheet closed, backdrop hidden; gapped control unchanged). **FIXED IN ROUND 20 (owner-approved Option A, 2026-10-10): the shared `caSheetGlideArm`/`caSheetGlideCancel` pair across all nine openers and nine closer sites; the pin block landed in dev-ui (8 pins, verified failing pre-fix); smoke ×3 with the fv tap pin 3/3. TASK CLOSED.**

Improvement #4 SHIPPED — see the entry titled "v1.43.0 round 18, improvement #4".

**v1.43.0 round 32 (owner order, 2026-10-11 — the nudge becomes a fixture: it stays in the chat until the crew closes it or the flight lands, and a landed sector of a multi-flight nudge leaves "just that line"; APP_VERSION stays 1.43.0, CACHE_NAME v213→v214; index.html + sw.js + next-flight + smoke + this entry):**

- **The order and its reading.** "Similar logic to What's New" = the nudge re-offers on every launch and survives in-session churn; the X remains the one permanent dismissal (per flight + date, unchanged); landing is the natural expiry. This supersedes two round-31 behaviours: H's saved-elsewhere collapse (a saved flight no longer leaves the list) and the done card's session-only life (a reload now rebuilds it).
- **What changed.** (1) `menuNudgeFlights` drops the `menusSavedFor` filter — an all-saved MULTI set renders straight into the done form (ok pills, inert green Saved CTA, "Menus saved for N flights — tap one to open."), and a saved SINGLE keeps its live Open-menus door with the "menus saved, available offline for the flight" copy (it opens from cache, offline included). (2) Offline, the offer is still never CREATED (the round-31 I ruling holds) — but a card already in the feed is never stripped: renderMenuNudge returns before touching it, because the saved-rows launcher is most useful with no signal. (3) Background tabs: a re-cast (the flight set grew) waits for the chat tab; landed-row prunes still run anywhere. (4) The nfBusy mid-sweep guard is unchanged. (5) Landing: the card stamps its flight set (`data-nfFlights`, plus `data-nfSaved` for the single-flight flip); a refresh against a SHRINKING set prunes surgically — `nfNudgeRowAway` collapses just that line (280ms, padding included), the headline re-counts through the new shared `nfNudgeCopyHtml`, and the card itself collapses (`nfNudgeCardAway` — the X animation factored out) only when the LAST flight lands. A growing or reshuffled set (a fresh roster import) or a single-flight save-flip re-casts in place (no re-animation, keeps its slot). `nextFlightExpirySweep` (60s + visibilitychange) drives the landing prune off the existing sta/staYmd logic — no new time math.
- **Pins.** next-flight 173→185 (+12): the saved-schedule block FLIPS contract — the nudge stays as the offline launcher (saved copy, live door, still no row for a single) — and a new lifecycle block pins the all-saved reload (done form, ok pills, inert receipt, NO transient confirm), the offline never-strips rule, and the landing story with a re-frozen clock + hand-run expiry sweep (landed line leaves, remaining line + re-counted copy stay, last landing removes the card). smoke 65→66 (+1): `setOfflineMode(true)` + `refreshNextFlightUi()` in real Chromium — the nudge survives.
- **Measurements.** index.html 17,758 → 17,836 lines (+78), 1,069,919 → 1,075,332 bytes. tw.css byte-identical (b8674cc969bde1fcebd8745166a73ef3 — no new utilities). app.html new floor: 1,074,159 bytes, md5 092e3d8819a6e3ec2eb76992b475088f.
- **Tests.** Battery 14/14 = **2,167 asserts**, 0 failed (next-flight 185; every other suite at its round-31 total). Smoke **66/66 first run**, zero page errors, zero console errors.
- **Census.** The `^\*\*` count moves 128→129 with this entry's title.

**v1.43.0 round 33 (owner bug report, 2026-10-11 — the calendar's first open per session showed blank space below the grid on the PC (fine on iPhone 11 and both Magic V3 screens; resizing did nothing; a close+reopen fixed it); APP_VERSION stays 1.43.0, CACHE_NAME v214→v215; index.html + sw.js + roster-calendar + smoke + this entry):**

- **The hunt (eleven rig diags, none of which could reproduce the blank head-on).** The stub rig opened clean at every viewport; the queue-race (a tap landing in a between-messages chat pause) opened clean; the real index.html with the real lucide UMD (fetched from npm, served late via request interception) opened clean; dpr 1.25, today outside the span, overnight-heavy and 3-month multi-leg rosters all converged. Two rig facts mattered along the way: the smoke server's What's New/chat-busy boot gate, and — decisively — that every earlier diag had seeded duty days with the WRONG SHAPE (`kind: 'F'` instead of the parser's `kind: 'fly'` + `dkey`), so no diag had ever exercised fly rows, icon swaps, or heterogeneous heights.
- **The mechanism (found by rig telemetry on the reveal's anchor dance, not by reproduction of the blank itself — three interacting defects, each individually logged).** (1) On a fresh page the detail is EMPTY, so the engine CLAMPS the reveal's very first `scrollTop = target` write back to 0; the dance's correction was based on the TARGET (`target + delta`), so it double-counted the whole offset — target plus the row's distance from a scroller that never moved — and hurled the window past row-land into spacer-only territory (rig log: `target=824, scrollTop=0, delta=627 → next=1450.5` → row lost ×3 → budget exhausted). (2) `rosterCalTLCover`'s re-derivation extrapolates the top index across unmounted territory by dividing the gap by the NEAREST MOUNTED row's height — a 41px empty day standing in for 300px fly cards — so the re-derived window could start BELOW the resting scroller, leaving the viewport inside the HEAD SPACER: zero rows in view, blank, and every later pass re-derived the same wrong window, so it sat there until a close+reopen. (3) Nothing anywhere asserted "the viewport must be showing a row" — the settle passes and the arm passes both re-ran the same exhausted dance instead of noticing the blank. Open #2 worked because the detail still holds the previous open's spacers (no empty-scroller clamp → no double-count → the dance converges from the persisted position), which is exactly the owner's first-open-only signature; resize does nothing because the window only re-derives on scroll/tap/open, never on resize.
- **The fix (three guarantees, one per link; all in the calendar's existing machinery, no new surfaces, both themes unaffected).** (1) The reveal's correction now bases itself on where the scroller ACTUALLY rests — `(det.scrollTop || 0) + delta` — identical whenever the scroller stuck, and the row's true offset whenever the engine clamped; the dance's budget widens 4→8 and it only gives up when genuinely stuck at its own target. (2) The window (both the main derivation and the post-correction second application) now UNIONS the cover window with the estimate window — the estimate always covers the scroller's own position, so no window can ever start below the scroller or end above it; the head-spacer blank is structurally impossible. (3) Each arm pass (90/360/760ms, unchanged timers) force-rebuilds the window if the viewport shows no row at all — a spacer-only state heals inside the pass, layout engines only (jsdom's zero rects skip the check by design).
- **Pins.** roster-calendar 299→306 (+7): source pins for the corrected base, the stuck-only termination, the widened budget, both union sites, and the arm-pass self-heal; plus a behavioural spanning sweep — for resting positions 0/300/1200/999999 the rendered window must span the scroller (head spacer never starts below it, rows always mounted). Existing pins survive untouched: the rcRevealArm heal test, the [90, 360, 760] timer array, the pointerdown cancel, the close cancel, the settle pass. smoke 66→68 (+2): in real Chromium, the first open lands the selected day flush at the detail's top with rows in view, and a window re-derived at an arbitrary mid-land position spans the scroller.
- **Measurements.** index.html 17,836 → 17,874 lines (+38), 1,075,332 → 1,078,008 bytes. tw.css byte-identical (b8674cc969bde1fcebd8745166a73ef3 — no new utilities). app.html new floor: 1,076,829 bytes, md5 f6c6355a855c0bfbf89a65c52dad8a93. Rig proof beyond the suites: the previously-stuck G-profile (short span, fly-heavy before today, the exact telemetry case) now converges at t+40ms — before the first arm pass — with the selected row at 0/−1px and 5 rows in view, stable through t+3200ms and on reopen; the 3-month and overnight-heavy profiles stay converged.
- **Tests.** Battery 14/14 = **2,174 asserts**, 0 failed (roster-calendar 306; every other suite at its round-32 total). Smoke **68/68 first run**, zero page errors, zero console errors.
- **Census.** The `^\*\*` count moves 129→130 with this entry's title.

Per the owner's approval of the card ("type help and send"), help now belongs to the card; the tour keeps its other two triggers — "tour" and "show me around" — and the card carries the pointer, so nothing is lost. If the owner prefers the old split (help = tour, ? = card), it is a one-line revert.

**v1.44.0 feature 2 of 3 (owner-approved, 2026-10-13 — roster diff on re-import: the confirmation card reports what changed when a month that already exists is replaced; the version and cache bump follow in the batch's final commit; index.html + roster-pdf + this entry):**

- **The change.** Attaching a roster PDF answers in the chat with the confirmation card (file, month, trips · sectors, skipped days, flags) — and a re-import used to swap the month silently. The card now carries one honest line per replaced month, right under the summary: "Versus the October 2026 you had: 2 new duties (17, 24 Oct) · 1 removed (31 Oct) · rest unchanged." First imports stay silent (nothing to compare); an identical re-import says "no changes."; date lists longer than four truncate with a "+N more" tail; a fully replaced month simply counts both sides. Day-level and store-truth by design: every parsed day row is what dutyRemember files, so "duty" means "a day the calendar holds", whatever its kind — kind changes on a kept date are deliberately NOT reported (the approved copy is new/removed/rest, no "changed" bucket).
- **Mechanics.** handleRosterFiles snapshots the old duty buckets for the months being imported BEFORE any store write and passes them to renderRosterConfirm; the diff itself is a pure helper (`rosterImportDiffLines(oldByMonth, parsedDays)`) so tests drive it directly. The store-write order is untouched — dutyRemember still runs at Build, Discard still leaves the old month in place.
- **Pins.** roster-pdf 191→198 (+7): first-import silence, the removed-day line with kept count, the identical-re-import line, the exact approved three-part shape, the +N-more truncation, the card carrying the line end-to-end, and the no-old-months card staying silent.
- **Tests.** roster-pdf solo **198/198**, 0 failed. (The full battery + smoke run once, on the batch's final bump commit.)
- **Census.** The `^\*\*` count moves 132→133 with this entry's title.

- **The change.** On a PC, ← and → now change the roster calendar's month — the same path a chevron tap takes (the glide, the data-edge no-ops, the drag/slide landing all included: the branch simply clicks #ca-rc-prev / #ca-rc-next). Three disciplines, mirroring the Escape handler it lives beside: (1) the calendar must be the FRONTMOST sheet — the topmost check walks the same id order the Escape closers use, so arrows never drive a sheet buried under What's New, Settings, the archive or the menu viewer; (2) an input that owns the caret owns the key — arrows typed into the chat input (or any field) never touch the calendar; (3) the whole 300ms close glide stands down — the sheet parks (translate-y-full) the instant a close starts while the wrap only hides at +300ms, and the arrow branch honours the sheet, not just the wrap (the jsdom pin caught exactly this window). Escape's own behavior is byte-untouched.
- **Pins.** roster-calendar 306→312 (+6): ArrowRight glides to the next month and ArrowLeft glides back; at the first data month ArrowLeft is the grayed-chevron no-op; an arrow into the chat input changes nothing; with the calendar closed (and through the close window) the arrows do nothing at all.
